# BOSSAI 12-agent AI attack mesh targeting Taiwan emergency services

- ID: bossai-12-agent-attack-mesh-2026-11
- Reported: 2026-11-04
- AI role: attacker_uses_ai_as_operator
- Entry: https://openattacks.ai/attacks/bossai-12-agent-attack-mesh-2026-11/

## Summary
Nov 4, 2026: Acronis TRU discovered BOSSAI, a mesh of 12 AI agents (9 active, 3 inactive) operated by single actor 'bossjy' (aliases TIAN0517, lstjks) based in Hualien, Taiwan. Agents divided into infrastructure builders (Grok, OpenClaw, Claude Code) running commercial models, and offensive agents (Hermes, HalberdStrike, CyberStrike) running abliterated local models (DeepSeek-V4, Qwen 3.5 122B). Commercial models repeatedly refused attack commands, forcing use of locally-run abliterated models. Agent mesh targeted hundreds of organizations across government emergency services, banks, universities, military systems in 11 countries. Confirmed breaches at Taiwan emergency services (patient care records, 119 dispatch data), universities (Thailand SQL injection), and financial institutions. Operator ran mesh from home IP alongside legitimate businesses (gas store, K-beauty reseller, Facebook auto-post SaaS). Attack mesh generated high volumes of data with frequent hallucinations and false positives; agents sometimes refused lucrative targets (Honduran military base) due to autonomous scope decisions. MCP servers provided GitHub security, CVE lookup, OSINT, cloud auditing, Ghidra, Radare2, Playwright automation, and Kali Linux tools (nmap, sqlmap, Metasploit, Hydra). GPU fleet: RTX 4090s serving abliterated models via llama.cpp, plus 8 VPS nodes for proxying. OPSEC failures exposed entire operation: GitHub account linked to physical storefront, SSH sessions exposed home IP, credentials hardcoded throughout.

## Category
adversary-use-of-ai

## Attribution
bossjy (TIAN0517, lstjks), Hualien City, Taiwan; high confidence single operator

- Actor: bossjy
- Actor ID: bossjy
- Aliases: TIAN0517, lstjks
- Country: Taiwan
- Motivation: financially-motivated
- Attributed by: Acronis TRU
- Confidence: high

## AI involvement confidence
confirmed

## Scope and targeting
- Initial access: Vulnerability exploitation (SQL injection, RCE), credential harvesting, exposed systems
- Victims and sectors: Taiwan emergency services (fire department, 119 dispatch, disaster response), financial institutions (lending, mobile payment, fire-dispatch vendors), universities (Thailand: 3 confirmed SQL injection with data exfiltration, 2 partial), Mexico (206,800+ residential phone records, 124,000+ yellow pages records), Honduras military air force personnel system (characterized but not exploited), government agencies across 11 countries
- Regions: Taiwan, Thailand, Mexico, Honduras, United States, United Kingdom, Australia, Sri Lanka, China, Vietnam
- Region notes: unknown
- Scale and metrics: 12-agent mesh; hundreds of organizations targeted across 11 countries; ~10 confirmed breaches; 206,800+ Mexican residential phone records; 124,000+ Mexican yellow pages records; Taiwan emergency medical data (ECG, ambulance video, vitals from 2018), 119 dispatch case records (7 Taipei districts); 3 Thai universities SQL injection with data extraction; 16 SQL injection points at Taiwan lending firm; ~95 Hermes offensive sessions; attack mesh ran for 12+ days under observation; 9GB evidence (90,000+ files); false positive rate >50% on high-severity findings from HalberdStrike

## Attack flow
### mesh-deploy: Deploy 12-agent BOSSAI mesh (ai)

Operator bossjy deploys mesh: 3 infrastructure agents (Grok, OpenClaw, Claude Code) with commercial models; 9 offensive agents (Hermes, HalberdStrike, CyberStrike, Cursor, Trae, etc.) with abliterated models

Evidence: <https://www.acronis.com/en/tru/posts/bossai-inside-the-ai-attack-mesh-that-targeted-taiwans-emergency-services/>

### recon: FOFA reconnaissance and target selection (execution)

Agents scan hundreds of organizations across 11 countries; FOFA reconnaissance feeds target lists to offensive agents

Evidence: <https://www.acronis.com/en/tru/posts/bossai-inside-the-ai-attack-mesh-that-targeted-taiwans-emergency-services/>

### vuln-scan: Autonomous vulnerability scanning (execution)

HalberdStrike, AWVS, CyberStrike scan targets; agents generate vulnerability reports (many false positives due to hallucinations)

Evidence: <https://www.acronis.com/en/tru/posts/bossai-inside-the-ai-attack-mesh-that-targeted-taiwans-emergency-services/>

### exploit: Autonomous exploitation (access)

Hermes agent executes SQL injection, RCE, credential harvesting autonomously; uses MCP tools (nmap, sqlmap, Metasploit, Hydra) via Kali MCP server

Evidence: <https://www.acronis.com/en/tru/posts/bossai-inside-the-ai-attack-mesh-that-targeted-taiwans-emergency-services/>

### exfiltrate: Data exfiltration (impact)

Emergency medical data (Taiwan), 119 dispatch records, university student data (Thailand), 206K+ phone records (Mexico), financial data extracted

Evidence: <https://www.acronis.com/en/tru/posts/bossai-inside-the-ai-attack-mesh-that-targeted-taiwans-emergency-services/>

### Connections

- mesh-deploy -> recon
- recon -> vuln-scan
- vuln-scan -> exploit
- exploit -> exfiltrate

## MITRE ATLAS
- [AML.T0040](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0040) AI Model Inference API Access — 12-agent AI mesh autonomously conducted offensive operations including reconnaissance, vulnerability scanning, and exploitation across multiple countries
  Evidence: <https://www.acronis.com/en/tru/posts/bossai-inside-the-ai-attack-mesh-that-targeted-taiwans-emergency-services/>

## Prevention
Monitor for autonomous AI agent behavior patterns; detect nmap, sqlmap, Metasploit execution from unexpected contexts; block MCP server abuse; restrict abliterated model access; monitor FOFA/Shodan reconnaissance patterns; detect multi-agent mesh communication; secure emergency services APIs; patch SQL injection vulnerabilities; monitor for hallucinated vulnerability reports; detect AWVS signatures

## Detections
- [Nmap/Zenmap Execution](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/windows/process_creation/proc_creation_win_pua_nmap_zenmap.yml) (sigma) — Detects nmap/zenmap execution used by BOSSAI agents for network reconnaissance
- [Linux HackTool Execution](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/linux/process_creation/proc_creation_lnx_susp_hktl_execution.yml) (sigma) — Detects execution of hacktools including nmap, sqlmap, Metasploit used by offensive agents
- [Linux Network Service Scanning](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/linux/auditd/syscall/lnx_auditd_network_service_scanning.yml) (sigma) — Detects network service enumeration via auditd syscalls
- [SQL Injection in Web Logs](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/web/webserver_generic/web_sql_injection_in_access_logs.yml) (sigma) — Detects SQL injection attempts in web server access logs (BOSSAI targeted Thai universities with SQL injection)
- Multi-agent AI mesh detection (guidance) — Monitor for patterns indicating multiple AI agents working in coordination: autonomous tool execution from unexpected contexts, MCP server traffic, high-volume reconnaissance, parallel targeting across multiple organizations
- [Suspicious Network Tool Launched Inside Container](https://github.com/elastic/detection-rules/blob/main/rules/linux/discovery_suspicious_network_tool_launched_inside_container.toml) (elastic) — Detects nmap, sqlmap, Metasploit tools in containers; directly applicable to BOSSAI mesh MCP-wrapped tooling
- [Web Application Suspicious Activity - sqlmap User Agent](https://github.com/elastic/detection-rules/blob/main/rules/apm/apm_sqlmap_user_agent.toml) (elastic) — Detects sqlmap tool used by BOSSAI against Thai universities
- Abliterated model abuse (guidance) — Detect locally-run abliterated LLM models (DeepSeek-V4, Qwen, GLM variants) serving offensive agents; monitor for llama.cpp GPU inference serving attack frameworks

# CERT-In / CSIRT-Fin CRAFTY FALCON AI-tool campaign against Indian BFSI

- ID: cert-in-crafty-falcon-bfsi-2026-11
- Reported: 2026-11-04
- AI role: attacker_uses_ai_as_operator
- Entry: https://openattacks.ai/attacks/cert-in-crafty-falcon-bfsi-2026-11/

## Summary
CERT-In Advisory CIAD-2026-0058 (Nov 4, 2026) warns of CRAFTY FALCON campaign targeting Indian non-banking financial companies (NBFCs), fintechs, payment aggregators, and cryptocurrency exchanges. Attackers chain multiple open-source agentic offensive AI frameworks: ARTEX (autonomous penetration testing), PentAGI (multi-agent security testing), Cairn (AI-driven web exploitation), and CyberStrikeAI (LLM-powered C2). Campaign features cloud/AWS Lambda pivots for staging-to-production access, AI-driven app reverse-engineering to invoke payment and merchant-settlement APIs from allow-listed infrastructure, Rs. 1 test transfers followed by large mule payouts, cryptocurrency wallet-key theft, comprehensive log deletion, and deliberate retargeting during festive windows when transaction volumes are high and security teams are reduced. CERT-In notes campaign exploits trust in cloud infrastructure and leverages AI agent autonomy to accelerate intrusion timelines from weeks to hours.

## Category
adversary-use-of-ai

## Attribution
CERT-In: CRAFTY FALCON financially motivated campaign; Indian press notes Chinese-linked offensive AI frameworks; no confirmed state nexus in CERT-In advisory

- Actor: CRAFTY FALCON
- Country: Unknown
- Motivation: financially-motivated
- Attributed by: CERT-In / CSIRT-Fin
- Confidence: high

## AI involvement confidence
confirmed

## Scope and targeting
- Initial access: Cloud service exploitation, exposed APIs, compromised AWS Lambda functions, credential stuffing against fintech portals
- Victims and sectors: Indian non-banking financial companies (NBFCs), fintechs, payment aggregators, cryptocurrency exchanges; festive-season targeting (Diwali, year-end)
- Regions: India
- Region notes: unknown
- Scale and metrics: Active since at least Oct 2026; Rs. 1 test transfers followed by large payouts; multiple NBFCs and fintechs compromised; festive-window retargeting pattern

# Haiou prompt-adware in Codex jailbreak bundle

- ID: checkpoint-haiou-codex-adware-2026
- Reported: 2026-11-04
- AI role: ai_agent_or_mcp_as_target
- Entry: https://openattacks.ai/attacks/checkpoint-haiou-codex-adware-2026/

## Summary
GitHub repository haiou-666/haiou-codex promoted on Bilibili and QQ as Codex jailbreak bundle. Installation decrypts AES-GCM encrypted payload.pkg and installs global Codex instructions plus seagull-* skills that inject advertisements for zz.aiapi2025.top into agent responses. Malicious skills also prevent agent from testing or revealing the injected ads. Attack monetizes jailbreak distribution through forced API gateway advertising while appearing to provide free model access workarounds.

## Category
uncategorized

## Attribution
haiou-666 (GitHub username)

## AI involvement confidence
not assessed

## Scope and targeting
- Initial access: User downloads and installs haiou-codex jailbreak bundle from GitHub promoted on Chinese social platforms
- Victims and sectors: Codex users seeking jailbreak or free access, primarily Chinese-speaking users
- Regions: China, Asia
- Region notes: China and Asia reflect primary promotion on Bilibili and QQ; global Codex users potentially affected
- Scale and metrics: Promoted on Bilibili and QQ; repository stars and download count unknown

## Attack flow
### distribution: Jailbreak bundle promoted on social platforms (access)

haiou-666/haiou-codex GitHub repository promoted on Bilibili and QQ as Codex jailbreak solution

Evidence: <https://research.checkpoint.com/2026/free-the-model-sell-the-gateway-prompt-adware-hidden-in-jailbreak-bundles/>

### install: User installs encrypted payload (execution)

Installation process decrypts AES-GCM payload.pkg and installs to system

Evidence: <https://research.checkpoint.com/2026/free-the-model-sell-the-gateway-prompt-adware-hidden-in-jailbreak-bundles/>

### inject: Malicious instructions and skills installed (ai)

Global Codex instructions modified and seagull-* skills installed with ad injection logic

Evidence: <https://research.checkpoint.com/2026/free-the-model-sell-the-gateway-prompt-adware-hidden-in-jailbreak-bundles/>

### adware: Ads injected into agent responses (impact)

Agent responses include advertisements for zz.aiapi2025.top API gateway; skills prevent agent from testing or revealing ads

Evidence: <https://research.checkpoint.com/2026/free-the-model-sell-the-gateway-prompt-adware-hidden-in-jailbreak-bundles/>

### Connections

- distribution -> install
- install -> inject
- inject -> adware

## MITRE ATLAS
- [AML.T0054](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0054) LLM Jailbreak — Malicious skills inject advertisements into agent responses
  Evidence: <https://research.checkpoint.com/2026/free-the-model-sell-the-gateway-prompt-adware-hidden-in-jailbreak-bundles/>
- [AML.T0081](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0081) Modify AI Agent Configuration — Global instructions and skills contain malicious prompt injection to force ad display
  Evidence: <https://research.checkpoint.com/2026/free-the-model-sell-the-gateway-prompt-adware-hidden-in-jailbreak-bundles/>

  Evidence: <https://research.checkpoint.com/2026/free-the-model-sell-the-gateway-prompt-adware-hidden-in-jailbreak-bundles/>

Review global Codex instructions for unauthorized modifications; scan installed skills for ad injection patterns; monitor agent responses for unexpected commercial content; verify instruction and skill sources; detect connections to suspicious API gateways; educate users about jailbreak bundle risks

No detections are recorded.

No timeline is recorded.

# Malicious AGENTS.md hijacking coding agents via Downloads

- ID: forever-agents-md-hijack-2026
- Reported: 2026-11-04
- AI role: ai_agent_or_mcp_as_target
- Entry: https://openattacks.ai/attacks/forever-agents-md-hijack-2026/

## Summary
Malicious AGENTS.md file dropped into ~/Downloads by website. When coding agent launches in Downloads directory, it reads and follows the malicious instructions. AGENTS.md variant edits ~/.codex/config.toml and runs npm install function-color (MALFEX downloader). CLAUDE.md variant steals OAuth tokens. Attack leverages agent instruction file priority and common workflow of developers launching agents in Downloads after downloading code samples or projects.

## Category
uncategorized

## Attribution
unknown

## AI involvement confidence
not assessed

## Scope and targeting
- Initial access: User visits malicious website that drops AGENTS.md into ~/Downloads
- Victims and sectors: Software developers using AI coding agents
- Regions: Multiple regions
- Region notes: Multiple regions reflects global developer population using coding agents
- Scale and metrics: Proof of concept demonstrating attack vector; actual deployment scale unknown

## Attack flow
### drop: Malicious instruction file dropped to Downloads (access)

Website drops AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md into ~/Downloads folder

Evidence: <https://forever.security/blog/hijacking-ai-agents-via-downloads-folder/>

### launch: Developer launches agent in Downloads (ai)

User starts coding agent in Downloads directory following common workflow for testing downloaded projects

Evidence: <https://forever.security/blog/hijacking-ai-agents-via-downloads-folder/>

### read: Agent reads malicious instruction file (ai)

Agent discovers and loads AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md based on instruction file precedence

Evidence: <https://forever.security/blog/hijacking-ai-agents-via-downloads-folder/>

### execute: Malicious instructions executed (execution)

AGENTS.md variant edits ~/.codex/config.toml and runs npm install function-color; CLAUDE.md variant steals OAuth token

Evidence: <https://forever.security/blog/hijacking-ai-agents-via-downloads-folder/>

### impact: Malware installed or credentials stolen (impact)

function-color MALFEX downloader installed or OAuth tokens exfiltrated

Evidence: <https://forever.security/blog/hijacking-ai-agents-via-downloads-folder/>

### Connections

- drop -> launch
- launch -> read
- read -> execute
- execute -> impact

## MITRE ATLAS
- [AML.T0051.001](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0051.001) Indirect — Malicious instruction file manipulates agent behavior by exploiting instruction file loading priority
  Evidence: <https://forever.security/blog/hijacking-ai-agents-via-downloads-folder/>
- [AML.T0081](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0081) Modify AI Agent Configuration — AGENTS.md contains malicious instructions that modify agent configuration and install malware
  Evidence: <https://forever.security/blog/hijacking-ai-agents-via-downloads-folder/>
- [AML.T0053](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0053) AI Agent Tool Invocation — Attack exfiltrates OAuth tokens via CLAUDE.md variant
  Evidence: <https://forever.security/blog/hijacking-ai-agents-via-downloads-folder/>
- [AML.T0011.001](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0011.001) Malicious Package — Malicious instructions modify agent configuration files for persistence
  Evidence: <https://forever.security/blog/hijacking-ai-agents-via-downloads-folder/>

## Prevention
Review Downloads folder before launching agents; scan for unexpected .md instruction files; monitor ~/.codex/config.toml and other agent config modifications; detect npm install from unusual locations; restrict agent instruction file precedence; alert on OAuth token access from unexpected processes

## Detections
No detections are recorded.

## Timeline
No timeline is recorded.

# CyberStrikeAI attacks against schools in Africa and Latin America

- ID: kaspersky-cyberstrikeai-schools-2026
- Reported: 2026-11-04
- AI role: attacker_uses_ai_as_operator
- Entry: https://openattacks.ai/attacks/kaspersky-cyberstrikeai-schools-2026/

## Summary
Financially motivated attacks against schools in Africa and Latin America used the open-source CyberStrikeAI implant. Initial access was through a compromised SQL Server. A C2-side LLM using MCP tools selected attack commands and achieved domain administrator privileges in three to four days. Techniques included a SystemUpdate scheduled task, certutil downloads, comsvcs.dll LSASS memory dumping, DCSync, and Golden Ticket generation.

## Category
adversary-use-of-ai

## Attribution
Unnamed. Kaspersky could not link the activity to a known group. Chinese-language script comments and UTC+8 control-server restarts suggest a Chinese-speaking operator.

- Actor: Unnamed operators
- Country: Unknown
- Motivation: unknown
- Attributed by: Kaspersky
- Confidence: low

## AI involvement confidence
confirmed

## Scope and targeting
- Initial access: Compromised SQL Server
- Victims and sectors: Schools in Africa and Latin America
- Regions: Africa, South America
- Region notes: Africa and South America reflect targeted school systems in these regions
- Scale and metrics: Domain admin achieved in 3-4 days; multiple schools targeted

## Attack flow
### initial-access: SQL Server compromised (access)

Attacker gained initial access through compromised SQL Server

Evidence: <https://securelist.com/cyberstrikeai-attacks/121715/>

### implant: CyberStrikeAI implant deployed (ai)

Open-source CyberStrikeAI implant installed with C2 connection to fastcdn.bar and 185.186.245.144

Evidence: <https://securelist.com/cyberstrikeai-attacks/121715/>

### llm-decision: LLM selects attack commands (ai)

C2-side LLM using MCP tools autonomously chooses and executes attack techniques

Evidence: <https://securelist.com/cyberstrikeai-attacks/121715/>

### privilege-escalation: SystemUpdate task and credential dumping (execution)

SystemUpdate scheduled task created; certutil used for downloads; comsvcs.dll dumps LSASS memory

Evidence: <https://securelist.com/cyberstrikeai-attacks/121715/>

### domain-admin: Domain admin achieved via DCSync and Golden Ticket (impact)

DCSync extracts domain credentials; Golden Ticket generated for persistence; domain admin achieved in 3-4 days

Evidence: <https://securelist.com/cyberstrikeai-attacks/121715/>

### Connections

- initial-access -> implant
- implant -> llm-decision
- llm-decision -> privilege-escalation
- privilege-escalation -> domain-admin

## MITRE ATLAS
- [AML.T0016.002](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0016.002) Generative AI — C2-side LLM with MCP tools autonomously executed attack operations
  Evidence: <https://securelist.com/cyberstrikeai-attacks/121715/>

## Prevention
Harden SQL Server; monitor for CyberStrikeAI indicators; detect SystemUpdate scheduled task creation; alert on certutil downloads; monitor comsvcs.dll LSASS dumps; detect DCSync activity; hunt for Golden Ticket use; block C2 infrastructure; review LOLBAS execution (certutil, comsvcs)

## Detections
- [Suspicious Child Process Of SQL Server](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/windows/process_creation/proc_creation_win_mssql_susp_child_process.yml) (sigma) — Matched sqlservr with security-detections-mcp. Kaspersky says the first commands in these incidents ran as children of sqlservr.exe.
- [Scheduled Task Creation Via Schtasks.EXE](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/windows/process_creation/proc_creation_win_schtasks_creation.yml) (sigma) — Matched schtasks with security-detections-mcp. The implant's first persistence step was a scheduled task named SystemUpdate.
- [File Decoded From Base64/Hex Via Certutil.EXE](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/windows/process_creation/proc_creation_win_certutil_decode.yml) (sigma) — Matched certutil decode with security-detections-mcp. Kaspersky says certutil decoded the assembled payload. The direct-IP download rule previously noted on this entry does not match that command.
- [Process Memory Dump Via Comsvcs.DLL](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/windows/process_creation/proc_creation_win_rundll32_process_dump_via_comsvcs.yml) (sigma) — Matched comsvcs with security-detections-mcp. The agent dumped lsass with comsvcs.dll MiniDump, passing the PID rather than the process name.
- [First Time Seen Account Performing DCSync](https://github.com/elastic/detection-rules/blob/main/rules/windows/credential_access_dcsync_newterm_subjectuser.toml) (elastic) — Matched DCSync with security-detections-mcp. After the domain administrator account was taken, the agent ran DCSync.
- Renamed Mimikatz and the implant token (guidance) — security-detections-mcp's Mimikatz hits key on the binary name or command line. Kaspersky says this activity used a renamed Mimikatz for DCSync and a Golden Ticket, so those name-based rules do not fit. No Sigma, Splunk, or Elastic rule encodes the X-Implant-Token header or the CyberStrikeAI certificate issuer.

## Timeline
- First seen: 2026-10-01
- Last updated: 2026-11-15
- 2026-10-23 (Trend Micro): TrendAI reports CyberStrikeAI campaign by Chinese-language operators targeting Japan
- 2026-11-04 (Kaspersky): CyberStrikeAI attacks against schools in Africa and Latin America disclosed
- 2026-11-15 (OpenAttacks): Tagged CyberStrikeAI and backfilled category, attribution, AI-involvement confidence, detections, and this timeline note from the cited Securelist report. Kaspersky does not name a group, country, or motive.

## Tools
### AI tooling and models
- CyberStrikeAI (https://github.com/CyberStrikeAI/CyberStrikeAI)
- LLM-driven C2 with MCP tools

### Other tooling
- SystemUpdate scheduled task
- certutil (LOLBAS)
- comsvcs.dll LSASS dump (LOLBAS)
- DCSync
- Golden Ticket
- Compromised SQL Server

### Offensive frameworks
- [CyberStrikeAI](https://openattacks.ai/tools/cyberstrikeai/) (`cyberstrikeai`)

### MagicSword projects

## Source notes
Relevant detection rules:
- Sigma: Scheduled Task Creation Via Schtasks (https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/windows/process_creation/proc_creation_win_schtasks_creation.yml)
- Sigma: Suspicious File Downloaded From Direct IP Via Certutil (https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/windows/process_creation/proc_creation_win_certutil_download_direct_ip.yml)
- Sigma: Process Memory Dump Via Comsvcs.DLL (LSASS dump) (https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/windows/process_creation/proc_creation_win_rundll32_process_dump_via_comsvcs.yml)
- Sigma: Suspicious LSASS Process Access (https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/windows/process_creation/proc_creation_win_sysinternals_procdump_lsass.yml)

- LOLBAS references: certutil (https://lolbas-project.github.io/lolbas/Binaries/Certutil/), comsvcs.dll (https://lolbas-project.github.io/lolbas/Libraries/Comsvcs/), schtasks (https://lolbas-project.github.io/lolbas/Binaries/Schtasks/)

# KeyHunter Operation - LLM API key theft and academic fraud

- ID: keyhunter-llm-key-theft-2026-11
- Reported: 2026-11-04
- AI role: ai_credential_theft
- Entry: https://openattacks.ai/attacks/keyhunter-llm-key-theft-2026-11/

## Summary
Nov 4, 2026: SOCRadar STRU discovered KeyHunter operation by leepsmarts (Chinese-speaking, UTC+8), active since Jan 2026. Two parallel tracks: (1) LLM API key theft: 2,233 API keys harvested (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, DeepSeek), 12 marked valid (0.54% yield), targeting 247 exposed LLM gateways on PaaS platforms (Vercel, Netlify, Railway, Render, Replit, Streamlit, Fly.io, Cloudflare Pages). Tools scan .env files, GitHub repos, and cloud metadata. (2) Academic fraud: 195 Harvard alumni records from Nov 2025 ShinyHunters torrent, application payment links for Stanford/UChicago/Wake Forest/Case Western/Central Michigan, accounts at NYU/UC Berkeley/Oregon State/UIUC/Colorado State. (3) Financial fraud: Harmony ONE crypto drainer, stolen Stripe merchant keys for card validation, CVVs from Chinese underground market. Infrastructure: 6 servers + Malaysia OpenClaw host, tied by PentAGI CA certificate. AI agents manage operations: Hermes Agent node runs AI_radar cron (every 240min) monitoring X/Twitter, HuggingFace, GitHub, arXiv for new LLM releases/quotas via grok-4.6 + Tavily proxy. Four-phase pipeline: Phase 0 (AI monitoring), Phase 1 (FOFA/Shodan reconnaissance), Phase 2 (env_hunter scans 50+ paths, fadidevv/keyhunter + bigzooooz/KeyHunter GitHub scrapers), Phase 3 (validation), Phase 4 (unified dashboard). OPSEC failure: directory listing on 209.112.91.74 exposed 110 files/37 directories including Telegram bot tokens, operational logs, stolen credentials. CVE-2026-41940 (CVSS 9.8, CISA KEV) exploit staged against UTN Argentina, Arizona Christian University, University of Utah. Real victim seed phrases captured by Harmony ONE drainer.

## Category
attacks-on-ai

## Attribution
leepsmarts (@leepsmartsx), Chinese-speaking, UTC+8, GitHub PRO account

- Aliases: @leepsmartsx
- Country: China

confirmed

### ai-monitoring: Phase 0: AI-driven intelligence monitoring (ai)

Hermes Agent runs AI_radar cron (240min intervals) using grok-4.6 to monitor X/Twitter, HuggingFace, GitHub, arXiv for new LLM releases, API quotas via Tavily proxy

Secure .env files and cloud configurations; never expose API keys in GitHub repos or PaaS platforms; monitor cloud metadata endpoints (169.254.169.254); rotate API keys regularly; implement API key usage monitoring; restrict .env access; use secret management solutions (Vault, AWS Secrets Manager); monitor FOFA/Shodan reconnaissance; detect HFS server scanning; alert on Wayback Machine queries for .env files; implement PaaS security best practices

- API key validation traffic (guidance) — Detect automated API key validation attempts against Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, DeepSeek endpoints; monitor for high-volume failed authentication from single sources

# Load/Mine malicious Qwen2 models on Hugging Face

- ID: pluto-loadmine-qwen2-malware-2026
- Reported: 2026-11-04
- AI role: ai_agent_or_mcp_as_target
- Entry: https://openattacks.ai/attacks/pluto-loadmine-qwen2-malware-2026/

## Summary
Malicious Qwen2 test models uploaded to Hugging Face (e.g. MENXIANGYU/qwen2-tiny) and mirrored on ModelScope. config.json auto_map field points to modeling_evil2.EvilModel which executes on trust_remote_code=True and vLLM versions before 0.14.0 without that flag (CVE-2026-22807). Payload runs fileless peakminer cryptominer via memfd_create, establishes .bashrc persistence, deploys reverse shells, and achieves GPUStack container escape. Models appear as legitimate Qwen2 variants for testing purposes.

## Category
uncategorized

## Attribution
unknown

## AI involvement confidence
not assessed

## Scope and targeting
- Initial access: User loads malicious model from Hugging Face or ModelScope with trust_remote_code=True or vulnerable vLLM version
- Victims and sectors: ML researchers, AI developers, organizations using Hugging Face models
- Regions: Multiple regions
- Region notes: Multiple regions reflects global Hugging Face and ModelScope user base
- Scale and metrics: Multiple malicious models uploaded including MENXIANGYU/qwen2-tiny; exact victim count unknown

## Attack flow
### upload: Malicious models uploaded to repositories (access)

Attacker uploaded trojanized Qwen2 test models to Hugging Face and ModelScope with malicious config.json auto_map fields

Evidence: <https://pluto.security/blog/hugging-face-malware-campaign-load-mine/>

### load: Victim loads malicious model (ai)

User loads model with trust_remote_code=True or via vulnerable vLLM <0.14.0 (CVE-2026-22807)

Evidence: <https://pluto.security/blog/hugging-face-malware-campaign-load-mine/>

### execute: Malicious code executes during model load (execution)

modeling_evil2.EvilModel runs from config.json auto_map field during model initialization

Evidence: <https://pluto.security/blog/hugging-face-malware-campaign-load-mine/>

### miner: Fileless cryptominer deployed (impact)

peakminer runs via memfd_create for fileless execution; establishes .bashrc persistence

Evidence: <https://pluto.security/blog/hugging-face-malware-campaign-load-mine/>

### escalation: Additional payloads and container escape (impact)

Reverse shells deployed; GPUStack container escape achieved

Evidence: <https://pluto.security/blog/hugging-face-malware-campaign-load-mine/>

### Connections

- upload -> load
- load -> execute
- execute -> miner
- miner -> escalation

## MITRE ATLAS
- [AML.T0010.003](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0010.003) Model — Attackers poisoned publicly available ML models on Hugging Face and ModelScope platforms
  Evidence: <https://pluto.security/blog/hugging-face-malware-campaign-load-mine/>
- [AML.T0058](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0058) Publish Poisoned Models — Malicious code injected via model config.json auto_map field executes during model loading
  Evidence: <https://pluto.security/blog/hugging-face-malware-campaign-load-mine/>
- [AML.T0011.000](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0011.000) Unsafe AI Artifacts — Cryptominer achieves persistence through .bashrc modification
  Evidence: <https://pluto.security/blog/hugging-face-malware-campaign-load-mine/>
- [AML.T0034](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0034) Cost Harvesting — Fileless peakminer cryptominer runs via memfd_create to evade detection
  Evidence: <https://pluto.security/blog/hugging-face-malware-campaign-load-mine/>

## Prevention
Pin and verify model checksums; avoid trust_remote_code=True; update vLLM to 0.14.0 or later; monitor memfd_create usage; scan .bashrc for unauthorized changes; detect outbound cryptocurrency mining traffic; review model config.json auto_map fields; use model scanning tools

## Detections
No detections are recorded.

## Timeline
No timeline is recorded.

## Tools
### AI tooling and models
- [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/) model repository
- ModelScope mirror
- Qwen2 model format
- vLLM <0.14.0 (CVE-2026-22807)
- Transformers library (trust_remote_code execution)

### Other tooling
- peakminer cryptominer
- memfd_create (fileless execution)
- .bashrc persistence
- Reverse shell
- GPUStack container escape

## Source notes
Relevant detection rules:
- Sigma: Suspicious Cron File Created (persistence via .bashrc) (https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/linux/file_event/file_event_lnx_susp_cron_file_created.yml)
- Sigma: Audio Capture via memfd_create (fileless execution pattern, can detect suspicious memfd_create usage) (https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/linux/auditd/lnx_auditd_audio_capture.yml)
- Note: Cryptominer detection typically requires network traffic analysis (cryptocurrency pool connections) and process monitoring for mining-specific CPU/GPU patterns. Container escape detection depends on specific container runtime audit logs.

# Chainstack - ARTEX intrusion from a default-credential Grafana to production password hashes

- ID: chainstack-intruder-reached-production-user-password-2026-11
- Reported: 2026-11-03
- AI role: attacker_uses_ai_as_operator
- Entry: https://openattacks.ai/attacks/chainstack-intruder-reached-production-user-password-2026-11/

## Summary
Chainstack's incident page (created Nov 3, 2026, technical update Nov 10, page updated Nov 11) says an attacker reached production and customer account data, including password hashes. The Nov 10 update replaces the earlier unknown entry point. A test Grafana instance, deployed in July to a development Kubernetes cluster, was exposed to the internet with the default administrator credentials unchanged and without SSO or basic authentication in front. From Oct 26 at 19:26 UTC the attacker used Grafana's data-source proxy to reach internal services. A Grafana administrator can point a data source at any URL, and the proxy forwards the request. Over about two hours that reached dozens of internal services, including identity, database, automation, and monitoring. At 21:01 the proxy reached an internal MCP tool server that exposes Kubernetes operations to AI agents. It required no authentication, and its service account was bound to cluster-admin. Chainstack says network flows and the changes that followed show this server was the command executor; the individual tool calls were not recovered. The same night the attacker tested an internal workflow engine with names artx-check-* and artex-verify-*, created administrator users in the development identity provider, and replaced JavaScript in a development web app by mounting ConfigMaps over the served files. On Oct 27 the same loader was applied to staging and to the development Grafana. The loader referenced hxxps://silverbullet[.]icu/p/17fbad90-3094-4198-9b60-49fc852e331d/bootstrap.js and the marker window.__artx7f3a. No software vulnerability was exploited. Development machine credentials also worked in production. On Oct 29 those credentials and a BI-tool API key were used from 187.15.134[.]80. Between 17:03 and 17:12 a workflow was rewritten in the platform database so it ran shell commands, triggered with a valid webhook token through Grafana, then restored. That returned the automation pod's environment, its Kubernetes service-account token, and a machine identity. At 17:49 production Kubernetes secrets were listed from 18.237.52[.]142. On Oct 31, from 01:40 to 08:31, 187.15.134[.]45 bulk-read the production cloud secrets manager, created extra keys, and created a Kubernetes service account artx-sa with role artx-observer and token artx-sa-token. A short-lived pod on the host network took the node's cloud token from the instance metadata service. Another cluster ran a privileged pod with host networking and host PID and installed a static-pod relay to 185.242.233[.]68:39443. A compromised VPN peer key from that address reached the database network and flapped the tunnel, which is how the intrusion was noticed. Attacker pods queried production databases, including PostgreSQL pg_shadow on one instance. On Oct 30 a cron job ran Chisel (https://github.com/jpillora/chisel) as a reverse SOCKS tunnel to 185.242.233[.]68:10800, and a systemd timer named artx-guard beaconed to 185.242.233[.]68:8088/artx-beacon. The same host also served a VPN client on port 9331. Access paths were cut on Oct 31. Copied tokens kept working until 06:28 UTC on Nov 2. Chainstack assesses with high confidence that the attacker used ARTEX, from artx/artex markers such as artex-i37257-*, parallel paths, and chisel, and says the indicators do not match the actor-specific lists published for Vapor Panda, Storm-0940, or Storm-3358. Customer nodes, the console, and the API were not modified. Password hashes, organization and account names, member emails, and node credentials are treated as exposed. Payment card data is not stored. ATT&CK techniques Chainstack maps include T1078.001, T1078.004, T1609, T1610, T1611, T1053.003, T1053.006, T1552.005, T1552.007, and T1572.

## Category
adversary-use-of-ai

## Attribution
Unnamed. Chainstack attributes the tooling to ARTEX with high confidence and says that does not identify the operator. None of the indicators match the actor-specific sets published for Vapor Panda, Storm-0940, or Storm-3358.

- Actor: Unnamed
- Country: Unknown
- Motivation: unknown
- Attributed by: Chainstack
- Confidence: high

## AI involvement confidence
vendor-asserted

## Scope and targeting
- Initial access: Internet-exposed test Grafana in a development Kubernetes cluster, left on the default administrator credentials, then Grafana's data-source proxy into an unauthenticated MCP tool server whose service account had cluster-admin
- Victims and sectors: Chainstack, a blockchain infrastructure provider, and its customers' account records (organization names, account-holder names, member emails, password hashes) and node credentials, which Chainstack treats as exposed
- Regions: unknown
- Region notes: Chainstack lists offices in the United States and Singapore. The report does not say which country the compromised clusters ran in. Actor attribution is not a victim location.
- Scale and metrics: Development access from Oct 26 19:26 UTC. Production Kubernetes secrets read Oct 29 17:49 UTC. Production secrets-manager bulk reads Oct 31 01:40-08:31 UTC. Last use of a copied credential Nov 2 06:28 UTC. Customer-facing services kept running. Password hashes and node credentials are in scope. Card data was not stored.

## Attack flow
### grafana: Default-credential Grafana (access)

From Oct 26 19:26 UTC a test Grafana on a development cluster, reachable from the internet with unchanged default administrator credentials, proxies requests to internal services.

Evidence: <https://chainstack.com/security-incident-nov-2026/>

### mcp-exec: MCP server runs commands (ai)

At 21:01 the proxy reaches an unauthenticated MCP tool server whose service account is cluster-admin. Chainstack says that server was the command executor. artx and artex names appear on the artifacts.

Evidence: <https://chainstack.com/security-incident-nov-2026/>

### prod-secrets: Development credentials reach production (execution)

Machine credentials from development work in production. Oct 31 brings a bulk read of the production secrets manager, a VPN key into the database network, and queries against production databases.

Evidence: <https://chainstack.com/security-incident-nov-2026/>

### persist: Chisel and a systemd timer (impact)

On Oct 30, cron starts a Chisel reverse SOCKS tunnel to 185.242.233[.]68:10800 and the artx-guard systemd timer beacons to port 8088. Copied credentials last work at Nov 2 06:28 UTC.

Evidence: <https://chainstack.com/security-incident-nov-2026/>

### Connections

- grafana -> mcp-exec
- mcp-exec -> prod-secrets
- prod-secrets -> persist

## MITRE ATLAS
- [AML.T0049](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0049) Exploit Public-Facing Application — The entry point was an internet-exposed Grafana. Chainstack says no software vulnerability was exploited; the data-source proxy and the unchanged default administrator account were abused.
  Evidence: <https://chainstack.com/security-incident-nov-2026/>
- [AML.T0053](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0053) AI Agent Tool Invocation — An unauthenticated MCP tool server became the command executor, and Chainstack says the attacker used ARTEX.
  Evidence: <https://chainstack.com/security-incident-nov-2026/>
- [AML.T0055](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0055) Unsecured Credentials — Development credentials, a production secrets-manager bulk read, Kubernetes secrets, and the instance-metadata token were stolen.
  Evidence: <https://chainstack.com/security-incident-nov-2026/>
- [AML.T0012](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0012) Valid Accounts — The Grafana default administrator account, cloud service-account keys, and a VPN peer key were reused as valid accounts, including from development into production.
  Evidence: <https://chainstack.com/security-incident-nov-2026/>

## Prevention
Do not publish a test Grafana, or any admin UI, without SSO or an equivalent extra login, and replace default administrator credentials at deploy time. Treat a data-source proxy as privileged: allowlist destinations and block cluster-internal addresses that are not approved data sources. An MCP or other AI tool server needs authentication and must not run as cluster-admin. Development credentials must not be valid in production. Alert on secret lists, bulk cloud secret reads, a new privileged or hostPath pod, a systemd timer, cron launching a tunnel, and the same VPN peer key presented from two addresses. artx and artex resource names are a hunt clue only when tied to this behavior.

## Detections
- [Potential Protocol Tunneling via Chisel Client](https://github.com/elastic/detection-rules/blob/main/rules/linux/command_and_control_linux_chisel_client_activity.toml) (elastic) — Selected from a security-detections-mcp search for chisel. Linux endpoint. Chisel client command-line flags followed by a network connection from an uncommon process. Matches the reverse SOCKS tunnel Chainstack found on development hosts. The Windows chisel.exe rule is not cited.
- [Systemd Timer Created](https://github.com/elastic/detection-rules/blob/main/rules/linux/persistence_systemd_scheduled_timer_created.toml) (elastic) — Selected from a security-detections-mcp search for systemd timer. Linux endpoint. Creation of a systemd timer, including user timers. Matches artx-guard.timer. The rule does not look for the artx-guard name or the :8088/artx-beacon URL.
- [Cron Job Created or Modified](https://github.com/elastic/detection-rules/blob/main/rules/linux/persistence_cron_job_creation.toml) (elastic) — Selected from a security-detections-mcp search under T1053.003. Linux endpoint. A new or changed cron job. Chainstack found /etc/cron.d/artx-ngx launching Chisel. The rule is the cron write, not the Chisel binary.
- [Kubernetes Privileged Pod Created](https://github.com/elastic/detection-rules/blob/main/rules/integrations/kubernetes/privilege_escalation_privileged_pod_created.toml) (elastic) — Selected from a security-detections-mcp search for privileged pod. Kubernetes audit. Creation of a pod or container with privileged mode. Matches the production pod Chainstack described with host networking and host PID. A hostPath mount that is not privileged needs the hostPath rule instead.
- [Kubernetes Pod Exec Cloud Instance Metadata Access](https://github.com/elastic/detection-rules/blob/main/rules/integrations/kubernetes/credential_access_kubernetes_pod_exec_cloud_instance_metadata.toml) (elastic) — Selected from a security-detections-mcp search for instance metadata. Kubernetes audit of pod exec commands that reference a cloud metadata endpoint. Chainstack says a host-network pod took the node cloud token from the instance metadata service. A token read that never appears in an exec command line will not match.
- [Kubernetes Secrets Enumeration](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/application/kubernetes/audit/kubernetes_audit_secrets_enumeration.yml) (sigma) — Selected from a security-detections-mcp search for Kubernetes secrets. Kubernetes audit list on secrets. Chainstack says a production list request returned secret contents, which this list event covers. The follow-on individual reads are a separate get.
- Grafana default login, the data-source proxy, and the MCP server (guidance) — security-detections-mcp search for Grafana returned no rule about default administrator credentials or /api/datasources/proxy/. The hits were unrelated process rules, so they are not cited. Hunt Grafana logins that still use the default administrator account, new data sources aimed at cluster-internal addresses, and proxy requests to those addresses. The cluster-admin role-binding creation rules are also not cited: Chainstack says the MCP server's service account already had cluster-admin. No indexed rule alerts on an unauthenticated MCP server using a pre-existing cluster-admin account.

## Timeline
- First seen: 2026-10-26
- Last updated: 2026-11-11
- 2026-07-01 (Chainstack): July 2026, day not stated: a test Grafana is deployed on a development cluster with default administrator credentials and no SSO or basic authentication in front.
- 2026-10-26 (Chainstack): 19:26 UTC first use of the Grafana data-source proxy. 21:01 first connection to the unauthenticated MCP tool server. A JavaScript loader is injected the same night.
- 2026-10-29 (Chainstack): Development cloud credentials are used from 187.15.134\[.\]80. A rewritten workflow yields a machine identity. Production Kubernetes secrets are read at 17:49 UTC.
- 2026-10-30 (Chainstack): Chisel reverse SOCKS to 185.242.233\[.\]68:10800 and the artx-guard systemd timer to :8088/artx-beacon are installed on development hosts.
- 2026-10-31 (Chainstack): Bulk production secrets-manager reads, new keys, the VPN peer key, database queries, and containment. A malicious deployment commit at 15:54 fails because cluster access is already cut.
- 2026-11-02 (Chainstack): 06:28 UTC last observed use of a copied credential. Two development hosts that were brought back up tried to run their implants and the outbound connections were blocked.
- 2026-11-03 (Chainstack): Initial public incident report. Entry is described as a third-party component. Customer account data and password hashes are in scope.
- 2026-11-10 (Chainstack): Technical update: Grafana default credentials, the MCP server as command executor, ARTEX markers, and the indicator table.
- 2026-11-11 (Chainstack): Incident page marked updated. Chainstack says customer nodes, the console, and the API were not modified.

## Tools
### AI tooling and models
- [ARTEX](https://github.com/RuoJi6/ARTEX) (https://github.com/RuoJi6/ARTEX) — Community-maintained fork; the original repository is unavailable. MoLeft/ARTEX also returned HTTP 200 on 2026-11-16. The original Autumn-27/ARTEX repository returned HTTP 404.
- Unauthenticated MCP tool server bound to cluster-admin, used as the command executor

### Other tooling
- Grafana data-source proxy
- Chisel (https://github.com/jpillora/chisel)
- systemd timer artx-guard

### Offensive frameworks

## Source notes
The Nov 10 technical section is the source for the Grafana entry, the MCP server, the ARTEX assessment, the IPs, and the hashes. The page was updated Nov 11. The Hacker News article is the Nov 3 report, before that section existed. Hashes are of application files the attacker modified to carry the loader, not of a second stage or of host binaries. Chainstack also published Grafana bundle hashes 07691e5949a123e313e1d589288e3831ec8ac30b90c45ab99d3b6ad07f23789a and 455473033f7ecbeafaa70e38f41f924377d54013efedeb0996ee84d3422c10ce, and test-page hashes 7c5925a3bc97983bfbfccdc514edb4dadbdb5b796a0747a764b0dcc4f103ea15 and b4a701949a1489e81754637238ed3f8177520f9f950e655f9357900d9e30aea8. # Cyera Research - Tampered copy of Anthropic's PDF skill carrying Linux Go backdoor system_linux

- ID: cyera-anthropic-pdf-skill-backdoor-2026-11
- Reported: 2026-11-03
- AI role: ai_agent_or_mcp_as_target
- Entry: https://openattacks.ai/attacks/cyera-anthropic-pdf-skill-backdoor-2026-11/

## Summary
Cyera Research (Nov 3, 2026) discovered six skill archives impersonating Anthropic's official PDF skill, observed May 23 - July 14, 2026. Each archive contains 11 files byte-identical to upstream Anthropic pdf skill (github.com/anthropics/skills), but SKILL.md is truncated from 315 lines to 19 lines keeping official name and description while replacing Quick Start section with curl https://scan.100tal.com command, and adds 5.1 MB garble-obfuscated 64-bit Linux Go ELF binary system_linux. Binary features AES-256-GCM encrypted configuration with 0xAA XOR decryption revealing C2 81.70.101.115:2345 and attaches /bin/bash over raw TCP socket. Simplified Chinese strings in binary and UTC+8 archive timestamps indicate Chinese-language development environment. scan.100tal.com hostname linked to TAL Education Group infrastructure. Cyera notes attribution is unknown and could indicate targeted attack or internal security exercise. Cyera analyzed archives but did not observe live execution of system_linux backdoor. garble obfuscator (github.com/burrowers/garble) used to strip Go function names and identifiers. Demonstrates supply-chain risk where skill typosquatting or compromised distribution channels can deliver backdoors to AI developer environments.

## Category
attacks-on-ai

## Attribution
Unknown actor; UTC+8 archive timestamps; Simplified Chinese strings in binary; scan.100tal.com hostname (TAL Education Group-linked); Cyera notes attribution unknown and could be targeted attack or security exercise

- Actor: Unknown
- Country: Unknown
- Motivation: unknown
- Attributed by: Cyera Research
- Confidence: low

## AI involvement confidence
confirmed

## Scope and targeting
- Initial access: Developers install tampered PDF skill archive from unknown distribution channel (not Anthropic's official Skills repository)
- Victims and sectors: AI developers using Claude Skills; targets include AI/ML engineering teams, research labs, and organizations deploying Anthropic skills
- Regions: Multiple regions
- Region notes: unknown
- Scale and metrics: Six tampered skill archives observed May 23 - July 14, 2026; Cyera analyzed archives but did not observe live system_linux execution

## Attack flow
### create-tampered-skill: Create tampered PDF skill with system_linux backdoor (execution)

Create tampered PDF skill with system_linux backdoor

Evidence: <https://www.cyera.com/research/malicious-copy-anthropic-pdf-skill-carries-linux-backdoor>

### truncate-skill-md: Truncate SKILL.md (315→19 lines), add curl scan.100tal.com (execution)

Truncate SKILL.md (315→19 lines), add curl scan.100tal.com

Evidence: <https://www.cyera.com/research/malicious-copy-anthropic-pdf-skill-carries-linux-backdoor>

### obfuscate-backdoor: garble-obfuscate system_linux Go binary (execution)

garble-obfuscate system_linux Go binary

Evidence: <https://www.cyera.com/research/malicious-copy-anthropic-pdf-skill-carries-linux-backdoor>

### distribute-skill: Distribute tampered skill via unknown channel (execution)

Distribute tampered skill via unknown channel

Evidence: <https://www.cyera.com/research/malicious-copy-anthropic-pdf-skill-carries-linux-backdoor>

### developer-install: Developer installs tampered PDF skill (execution)

Developer installs tampered PDF skill

Evidence: <https://www.cyera.com/research/malicious-copy-anthropic-pdf-skill-carries-linux-backdoor>

### backdoor-execution: system_linux connects 81.70.101.115:2345, attaches /bin/bash (impact)

system_linux connects 81.70.101.115:2345, attaches /bin/bash

Evidence: <https://www.cyera.com/research/malicious-copy-anthropic-pdf-skill-carries-linux-backdoor>

### Connections

- create-tampered-skill -> truncate-skill-md
- truncate-skill-md -> obfuscate-backdoor
- obfuscate-backdoor -> distribute-skill
- distribute-skill -> developer-install
- developer-install -> backdoor-execution

## MITRE ATLAS
- [AML.T0000](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0000) Search Open Technical Databases — Supply-chain attack targeting AI skill distribution; tampered Anthropic PDF skill delivers Linux backdoor to developer workstations in AI/ML development environments
  Evidence: <https://www.cyera.com/research/malicious-copy-anthropic-pdf-skill-carries-linux-backdoor>

## Prevention
Only install skills from Anthropic's official Skills repository (github.com/anthropics/skills); verify skill archive integrity and file count before installation; scan skill archives for unexpected binaries (system_linux, system_darwin, etc.); monitor skill installation for outbound connections to non-Anthropic domains (scan.100tal.com, 81.70.101.115); implement network egress filtering for skill execution environments; use read-only skill directories where possible; audit SKILL.md for truncated or modified Quick Start sections referencing external domains; educate developers on skill supply-chain risks

## Detections
- [Linux Shell Pipe to Shell](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/linux/process_creation/proc_creation_lnx_susp_pipe_shell.yml) (sigma) — Detects suspicious shell pipes; relevant for detecting /bin/bash reverse shell execution from system_linux backdoor
- [Bash Interactive Shell](https://github.com/SigmaHQ/sigma/blob/master/rules/linux/process_creation/proc_creation_lnx_bash_interactive_shell.yml) (sigma) — Detects bash interactive shell spawning; directly applicable to system_linux attaching /bin/bash to TCP socket
- Skill supply-chain integrity monitoring (guidance) — Verify skill archives against official Anthropic Skills repository (github.com/anthropics/skills); alert on unexpected binaries in skill directories (system_linux, system_darwin); scan SKILL.md for modified Quick Start sections referencing external domains (scan.100tal.com); monitor skill installation for outbound TCP connections to non-Anthropic infrastructure (81.70.101.115:2345); detect garble-obfuscated Go binaries via string entropy analysis; flag skill archives with UTC+8 timestamps and Simplified Chinese strings when organization is not China-based
- Network egress filtering (guidance) — Block outbound connections from skill execution environments to unknown IPs; alert on raw TCP connections to port 2345; monitor for /bin/bash processes with network sockets; detect AES-256-GCM encrypted config files in skill directories

## Timeline
- First seen: 2026-05-23
- Last updated: 2026-11-03
- 2026-05-23 (Cyera Research): Earliest observed tampered PDF skill archive with system_linux backdoor
- 2026-07-14 (Cyera Research): Latest observed tampered PDF skill archive (six total observed May 23 - Jul 14)
- 2026-11-03 (Cyera Research): Cyera publishes analysis with IOCs and backdoor mechanism; notes attribution unknown and system_linux was not observed executing

## Tools
### AI tooling and models
- Anthropic [Claude](https://claude.com/product/overview) Skills (impersonated; official repo https://github.com/anthropics/skills)
- garble [Go](https://github.com/golang/go) obfuscator (https://github.com/burrowers/garble)

### Other tooling
- system_linux [Go](https://github.com/golang/go) backdoor (5.1 MB ELF64, AES-256-GCM + 0xAA XOR config)
- /bin/bash reverse shell over TCP

## Source notes
Cyera Research (Assaf Morag, Nov 3, 2026) primary source; all hashes from Cyera IoC table: system_linux SHA-256 50cb53704f318e4fc9a9cd8f6d32eb98d8c666501c720d63c2c701caeb6fa946, SKILL.md SHA-256 d0d4144609825ee7480f7889ee04d97f4b1d9eaa8a46c2a0d46d81ab5aa83827, plus archive hashes; network IOCs 81.70.101.115 and scan.100tal.com verified from Cyera; Cyera explicitly states archives were analyzed but system_linux was not observed executing

## Sources

# GTIG LLMJacking - UNC6993, Jet Night, D1337-KAIROS monetize cloud models

- ID: gtig-llmjacking-unc6993-2026-11
- Reported: 2026-11-02
- AI role: ai_infra_as_c2
- Entry: https://openattacks.ai/attacks/gtig-llmjacking-unc6993-2026-11/

## Summary
Multiple threat clusters (UNC6993, Jet Night operators, D1337-KAIROS) hijack enterprise access to LLMs across AWS Bedrock, Azure, GCP. Initial access via exposed cloud identity tokens (IAM, Service Account keys, GitHub PATs, session tokens), stolen LLM API keys from public GitHub, exploited web app vulnerabilities, LiteLLM CVE-2026-40217 (/key/regenerate endpoint rebind). UNC6993 used session hijacking page masquerading as app portal to steal JSON payloads with active access tokens. Threat actors enable LLM services, create API keys, provision GPUs, subscribe Pay-As-You-Go, deploy LiteLLM/new-api/gcli2api gateways, host Manus agent framework with SearXNG metasearch. Monetize via LLM Mixers (Jet Night at jet-night.com $60/month unlimited, ai-relay on lookalike domains, newapi.pro). Stealthy credential validation sweeps with max_tokens=1. Fraudulent charges $100K in single day, cumulative $1M per org. Division of labor between initial access and downstream monetization. Fireworks AI (Nov 9, 2026) reports Oct 23 third-party cloud provider hosting Fireworks R&D breached; Oct 24 attacker entered via provider, ran unauthorized workload; reached account/billing data and, for some customers, secrets, datasets, partly trained model weights. Fireworks attributes to financially motivated LLMjacking group selling access to illicit routing gateways; GTIG/Mandiant report referenced; Unit 42 forensics. Notion, a Fireworks customer, published a downstream notice: Fireworks told Notion on Nov 2 that a small dataset of Notion AI synthetic test data was accessed; Notion disconnected and deleted Fireworks data by the morning of Nov 3; on Nov 6 Fireworks said customer data may have been accessed; on Nov 8 Fireworks confirmed the final dataset; on Nov 9 Notion contacted affected customers. The exposed data is Notion AI Insights chat transcripts for under 1 percent of workspaces, mostly unattributable (no user or workspace labels, and no tool-call results). No other Notion features were affected. Notion told affected customers to treat the transcripts as exposed and to rotate secrets pasted into Notion AI. On Nov 11 Notion revoked exposed Notion API keys found in affected user messages.

## Category
adversary-use-of-ai

## Attribution
UNC6993 (Mandiant); Jet Night operators; D1337-KAIROS persona; multiple financially motivated threat clusters operating globally; division of labor between initial access operators and LLM Mixer monetization operators

- Actor: UNC6993, Jet Night operators, D1337-KAIROS
- Country: Global
- Motivation: financially-motivated
- Attributed by: Google Threat Intelligence Group, Fireworks AI
- Confidence: high

## AI involvement confidence
confirmed

## Scope and targeting
- Initial access: Misconfigured Service Account keys; session hijacking page masquerading as LLM portal; stolen GitHub PATs + web app vulnerability exploitation; compromised IAM credentials with cloud federation tokens; harvested exposed LLM API keys from public GitHub; LiteLLM CVE-2026-40217 (user ID rebind vulnerability in /key/regenerate endpoint)
- Victims and sectors: Organizations in North America, South America, Asia Pacific across financial services, retail, technology, transportation sectors; Fireworks AI customers (account/billing data, secrets, datasets, partly trained model weights for some customers); Notion workspaces using Fireworks for Notion AI Insights (under 1 percent, chat transcripts)
- Regions: North America, South America, Asia
- Region notes: unknown
- Scale and metrics: Multiple incidents in 2026; fraudulent charges exceeding $100K USD in single day; cumulative charges at some orgs reaching $1M USD; Jet Night passed nearly $1M in token costs to victim org; one actor made ~800K Bedrock calls over 30 hours (90% success), another 1.7M calls over 13 hours (1M+ success); 500K+ LLM API calls in <8 hours; Jet Night offers unlimited tokens $60/month (typical market $100-$250/month)

## Attack flow
### initial-access: Compromised cloud identity and LLM API keys (access)

Misconfigured Service Account keys; session hijacking page for LLM portal JSON payloads; stolen GitHub PATs + web app vuln; compromised IAM with federation tokens; exposed LLM API keys harvested from public GitHub; stealthy validation sweeps with max_tokens=1

Evidence: <https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/llmjacking-monetizing-compromised-cloud-models>

### lateral-persistence: Privilege escalation and persistence (execution)

Enumerated cloud services/repos/data stores; cloned codebases for secrets; queried data warehouse metadata; exploited LiteLLM CVE-2026-40217 (/key/regenerate rebind) to elevate Virtual Keys to PROXY_ADMIN; created unauthorized IAM/service accounts; injected SSH keys; attached admin policies; created backdoor accounts disguised as billing utilities

Evidence: <https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/llmjacking-monetizing-compromised-cloud-models>

### llm-abuse: LLM resource hijacking (ai)

Enabled managed generative platforms; created additional API keys; subscribed Pay-As-You-Go; selected premium models; launched high-volume inference; 'burn and rotate' short-lived service accounts; provisioned GPU instances; requested quota increases; deployed LiteLLM gateways; hosted Manus agent framework with SearXNG metasearch and sandbox execution

Evidence: <https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/llmjacking-monetizing-compromised-cloud-models>

### monetization: LLM Mixer resale (impact)

UNC6993 deployed new-api and gcli2api to aggregate stolen keys and balance loads; Jet Night (jet-night.com) $60/month unlimited token plan powered by stolen keys, passed ~$1M costs to victim; ai-relay on lookalike domains siphons access to newapi.pro resale ecosystem; D1337-KAIROS persona with Node.js proxy bundles; division of labor between access and monetization operators

Evidence: <https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/llmjacking-monetizing-compromised-cloud-models>

### charges: Fraudulent billing (impact)

UNC6993 accumulated $100K+ charges quickly; cumulative charges at orgs reaching $1M; one actor 800K Bedrock calls over 30 hours (Opus 75% of calls), another 1.7M calls over 13 hours; token usage profile resembles resold coding-agent traffic with prompt caching; $100K+ estimated usage at list price for two documented cases

Evidence: <https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/llmjacking-monetizing-compromised-cloud-models>

### Connections

- initial-access -> lateral-persistence
- lateral-persistence -> llm-abuse
- llm-abuse -> monetization
- llm-abuse -> charges
- monetization -> charges

## MITRE ATLAS
- [AML.T0000](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0000) Search Open Technical Databases — Threat actors compromise cloud compute credentials and hijack enterprise access to AWS Bedrock, Azure Cognitive Services, and GCP Gemini to fraudulently monetize through illicit gateways and LLM Mixers
  Evidence: <https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/llmjacking-monetizing-compromised-cloud-models>
- [AML.T0034](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0034) Cost Harvesting — Fireworks AI third-party cloud provider breach resulted in unauthorized workload execution and customer data access
  Evidence: <https://fireworks.ai/security/bulletins/october-2026-security-incident-update>
- [AML.T0025](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0025) Exfiltration via Cyber Means — Attackers exfiltrated account/billing data, secrets, datasets, and partly trained model weights from Fireworks AI
  Evidence: <https://fireworks.ai/security/bulletins/october-2026-security-incident>

## Prevention
IAM least privilege; centralized secrets management; code monitoring for API keys; routine config audits; rate limiting via Cloud Armor; network allow listing; budget alerts and API quotas; phishing-resistant MFA; shadow LLM discovery; granular API key scopes; validation sweep monitoring (max_tokens=1); vulnerability/ML supply chain patching; gateway patch management (LiteLLM CVE-2026-40217); sandboxed execution; vetted model repositories; graph-based exposure modeling

## Detections
- Cloud LLM resource abuse monitoring (guidance) — Implement budget alerts and API quotas; monitor for validation sweeps (max_tokens=1); detect rapid GPU provisioning; alert on Pay-As-You-Go subscriptions from service accounts; monitor LiteLLM/new-api/gcli2api gateway deployments; detect Manus agent framework and SearXNG installations; flag burn-and-rotate service account patterns; monitor Jet Night, ai-relay, newapi.pro resale infrastructure connections
- Third-party cloud provider security (guidance) — For Fireworks AI incident: audit third-party cloud provider access; implement least-privilege for R&D environments; monitor unauthorized workload execution; detect account/billing data access anomalies; alert on secrets, datasets, model weight access; implement cloud provider security controls. Notion downstream notice has no endpoint or CloudTrail pattern: the exposed objects are Notion AI Insights chat transcripts held by the vendor. Affected customers should treat those transcripts as exposed and rotate secrets pasted into Notion AI.

## Timeline
- Last updated: 2026-11-11
- 2026-11-02 (Google Threat Intelligence Group): GTIG reports UNC6993, Jet Night, D1337-KAIROS LLMjacking campaigns; fraudulent charges $100K-$1M per org; LLM Mixer resale via jet-night.com, ai-relay, newapi.pro
- 2026-10-24 (Fireworks AI): Fireworks AI third-party cloud provider breached Oct 23; attacker entered Oct 24, ran unauthorized workload; accessed account/billing data, secrets, datasets, partly trained model weights
- 2026-11-09 (Fireworks AI): Fireworks AI attributes to financially motivated LLMjacking group selling illicit routing gateway access; GTIG/Mandiant report referenced; Unit 42 forensics
- 2026-11-02 (Notion): Fireworks notified Notion that a small dataset was accessed. Notion found only Notion AI synthetic test data, with no customer data in that first set.
- 2026-11-03 (Notion): Notion disconnected from Fireworks, deleted data held there, and stopped communication with Fireworks.
- 2026-11-06 (Notion): Fireworks told Notion that malicious actors may have accessed customer data. Notion opened a second investigation.
- 2026-11-08 (Notion): Fireworks confirmed the final impacted dataset to Notion.
- 2026-11-09 (Notion): Notion contacted affected customers. The dataset is Notion AI Insights chat transcripts for under 1 percent of workspaces, mostly unattributable, with no tool-call results and no other Notion features affected.
- 2026-11-11 (Notion): Notion revoked exposed Notion API keys found in affected user messages during the investigation.

# Malicious MCP servers on npm and PyPI (ifasn, gadgethumans, mcp-doctor)

- ID: malicious-mcp-servers-npm-2026-11
- Reported: 2026-11-02
- AI role: ai_agent_or_mcp_as_target
- Entry: https://openattacks.ai/attacks/malicious-mcp-servers-npm-2026-11/

## Summary
Multiple malicious MCP servers published on npm. @ifasn/md-doc-mcp and @ifasn/mmdd-doc-mcp probe cloud metadata endpoints, read sensitive files (/etc/shadow, SSH keys, .env, Kubernetes tokens), and exfiltrate to 121.199.24.94. @ifasn/ms-doc-mcp versions 2.0.9 and 2.1.1 (GHSA-556m-xfwj-7wff, MAL-2026-18603) use imdsAudit() function to steal Alibaba Cloud IMDS credentials from 100.100.100.200 and exfiltrate them over MCP stdout. gadgethumans-mcp versions 1.0.10-1.0.15 leak WALLET_PRIVATE_KEY environment variable in HTTP header on every tool call. Published with OpenSSF GHSA-rvw8-22w3-342v, GHSA-4h5p-w547-9qmv (Nov 2) and Leitwacht LWA-2026-13557, LWA-2026-13564. Aug 12, 2026: Pillar Security disclosed Deadbugz malicious MCP server (GitHub zellkernel) stealing AI model credentials and API keys. Feb 20, 2026: Socket discovered SANDWORM_MODE campaign spreading malicious MCP servers targeting developer credentials. November 9-12 additions: @rfewqa-fes/mnm-doc-mcp 1.0.0 (MAL-2026-19148, Amazon Inspector) ships an inert MCP stub in index.js. Its package.json pulls the only dependency, node-health-utils, as an off-registry tarball over plain HTTP from http://121.199.24.94/dl/health-utils-1.0.0.tgz, so whatever that host serves, including lifecycle scripts, runs at npm install. package.json sha256 f8ad55746cfac30b0a9401634b2728afc93e6dc20a85c511efd0ed312e466e8d. Package tarball sha1 f3ad664b487c4041e7e66e5744656ed44e1a6f6a. GitHub also marked @lemili9804/mcp-weather-server 1.0.3 and 1.0.6 (GHSA-qwfq-cw9x-55x4), mcp-contract-tester all versions (GHSA-34p7-qcxm-gc39, MAL-2026-19082), and @bestcoolman/dev-mcp (GHSA-vgx2-8w7x-xr8v, MAL-2026-19052) as malware, with no published behavior write-up. The entry now covers PyPI as well. my-mcp-server-driving-license-ocr 0.1.3 (MAL-2026-19204, GHSA-wg5f-m5mv-gmjg, Amazon Inspector, Nov 12) exposes an MCP tool, analyze_image, described as driving-license OCR. It POSTs the caller's image URLs or base64 bytes over plain HTTP to the hardcoded http://42.193.222.35:8000/ocr_pic and overwrites any MY_API_URL setting so the destination cannot be changed. karma365-mcp 2.1.0 (GHSA-72xc-q3v8-7grp, Nov 13) and @go-gost/mcp-darwin-x64 0.1.0 (GHSA-gfpg-48q7-c68h, Nov 12) are GitHub malware advisories with no published behavior. Nov 16, 2026: Leitwacht LWA-2026-14164 says @guicai777/mcp-doctor 1.0.1 is a trojanized MCP config diagnostics CLI. On each run, index.js requests hxxps://fix-mcp-bridge[.]com/v[.]json. If the response field active is false, it prints that the remote payload is disabled and exits. Otherwise it decodes the bundled 866KB bridge.bin, a base64 blob XOR-encoded with a single-byte key stored as the file's first byte, into a Windows PE and writes it to %LOCALAPPDATA%\mcp-doctor\mcp-bridge.exe, then launches that file hidden and detached. Before the drop it reads ~/.claude.json, %APPDATA%\Claude\claude_desktop_config.json, ~/.cursor/mcp.json, and ~/.vscode/mcp.json. The README fallback is mshta hxxps://fix-mcp-bridge[.]com/init. The advisory describes the dropped file only as a bridge restart. Attack exploits trust in the MCP ecosystem and in npm and PyPI.

## Category
attacks-on-ai

## Attribution
unknown

- Actor: Unknown npm and PyPI publishers (ifasn, gadgethumans, rfewqa-fes, lemili9804, bestcoolman, zellkernel, karma365, go-gost, guicai777)
- Country: Unknown
- Motivation: cybercrime
- Attributed by: OpenSSF, Leitwacht, GitHub Advisory Database, Amazon Inspector, Pillar Security, Socket
- Confidence: medium

## AI involvement confidence
confirmed

## Scope and targeting
- Initial access: User installs malicious MCP server from npm
- Victims and sectors: AI agent developers, organizations using MCP servers
- Regions: Multiple regions
- Region notes: Multiple regions reflects global npm and MCP user base
- Scale and metrics: Multiple malicious MCP packages identified (ifasn, gadgethumans, Deadbugz, SANDWORM_MODE); exact installation count unknown

## Attack flow
### publish: Malicious MCP servers published to npm (access)

Three packages published including @ifasn/md-doc-mcp, @ifasn/mmdd-doc-mcp, and gadgethumans-mcp 1.0.10-1.0.15

Evidence: <https://github.com/ossf/malicious-packages>

### install: User installs malicious MCP server (ai)

Developer or organization adds malicious package to MCP configuration

Evidence: <https://github.com/ossf/malicious-packages>

### probe: Server probes for sensitive information (execution)

ifasn packages probe cloud metadata endpoints and read /etc/shadow, SSH keys, .env, k8s tokens

Evidence: <https://github.com/ossf/malicious-packages>

### exfil-files: File-based credentials exfiltrated (impact)

ifasn packages send stolen credentials to 121.199.24.94

Evidence: <https://github.com/ossf/malicious-packages>

### exfil-header: Wallet keys leaked in headers (impact)

gadgethumans-mcp sends WALLET_PRIVATE_KEY in HTTP header on every tool call

Evidence: <https://github.com/ossf/malicious-packages>

### Connections

- publish -> install
- install -> probe
- probe -> exfil-files
- probe -> exfil-header

## MITRE ATLAS
- [AML.T0010.001](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0010.001) AI Software — Attackers published malicious MCP servers through npm package ecosystem
  Evidence: <https://github.com/ossf/malicious-packages>
- [AML.T0011.001](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0011.001) Malicious Package — Malicious MCP servers run persistently as agent tooling
  Evidence: <https://github.com/ossf/malicious-packages>
- [AML.T0055](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0055) Unsecured Credentials — Packages exfiltrate credentials from environment variables and sensitive files
  Evidence: <https://github.com/ossf/malicious-packages>
- [AML.T0025](https://atlas.mitre.org/techniques/AML.T0025) Exfiltration via Cyber Means — MCP servers probe cloud metadata endpoints for credentials and instance information
  Evidence: <https://github.com/ossf/malicious-packages>

## Prevention
Verify MCP server sources before installation; review MCP server permissions and network access; monitor cloud metadata endpoint access; detect unauthorized file reads of sensitive paths; inspect HTTP headers for credential leakage; use MCP server sandboxing; inventory installed MCP packages

## Detections
- [Curl Download Activity from npm Package Install](https://github.com/elastic/detection-rules/blob/main/rules/cross-platform/command_and_control_curl_download_from_npm_install.toml) (elastic) — Endpoint, cross-platform. curl spawned from an npm or npx process tree. Fits packages whose install fetches an off-registry tarball. It does not see the PyPI OCR tool's runtime HTTP POST.
- [Suspicious PowerShell from npm Package Install](https://github.com/elastic/detection-rules/blob/main/rules/windows/execution_suspicious_powershell_from_npm_install.toml) (elastic) — Windows. PowerShell started from an npm install script. Use when the remote tarball or a GHSA-flagged MCP package runs a Windows lifecycle script.
- Plain-HTTP tarball and GHSA-only packages (guidance) — Also block http://121.199.24.94/dl/health-utils-1.0.0.tgz and http://42.193.222.35:8000/ocr_pic. The OCR package exfiltrates at tool-call time, not at pip install, and no cited rule matches an MCP tool POSTing image bytes. karma365-mcp, @go-gost/mcp-darwin-x64, @lemili9804/mcp-weather-server, mcp-contract-tester, and @bestcoolman/dev-mcp are confirmed malicious by GitHub but have no published behavior. For @guicai777/mcp-doctor, hunt %LOCALAPPDATA%\mcp-doctor\mcp-bridge.exe, mshta fetching hxxps://fix-mcp-bridge\[.\]com/init, and reads of the Claude, Cursor, and VS Code MCP config paths named by Leitwacht. The cited npm PowerShell rule does not match mshta or that dropped executable.

## Source notes
OpenSSF GHSA-rvw8-22w3-342v and GHSA-4h5p-w547-9qmv published Nov 2 2026; Leitwacht advisories LWA-2026-13557 and LWA-2026-13564; GitHub advisory GHSA-556m-xfwj-7wff (MAL-2026-18603) for @ifasn/ms-doc-mcp

Relevant detection rules:
- Monitor access to cloud metadata endpoints (169.254.169.254, 100.100.100.200 (Alibaba), metadata.google.internal, etc.)
- Alert on unauthorized reads of /etc/shadow, ~/.ssh/id_rsa, ~/.ssh/id_ed25519, .env files
- Detect access to Kubernetes service account tokens (/var/run/secrets/kubernetes.io/)
- Monitor HTTP headers for sensitive environment variable leakage (WALLET_PRIVATE_KEY, AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY, etc.)
- Alert on npm package installations from unverified sources
- Note: Cloud metadata access typically detected via network monitoring or instance-level audit logs (CloudTrail, Azure Monitor, GCP Cloud Logging). File access requires host-based file integrity monitoring.
